\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t))-\mathrm{B}(t,\mathbf{Y}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\sqrt{L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{A_{\delta,n}}\sqrt{L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)}\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\mathrm{d}t+\delta\int_{A_{\delta,n}^{c}}\sqrt{L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)}\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t

(4.33)

\displaystyle\leq 2K\bigg(\int_{A_{\delta,n}}L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\bigg).

Note that the function
L_{\mathrm{B}}\in\mathrm{L}^{1}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{+})
. Using the absolute continuity of the Lebesgue integral, we obtain

(4.34)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{A_{\delta,n}}L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)\mathrm{d}t=0.

To estimate the final term in the right hand side of (
4.1
), we use Hypothesis
3.4
(H.7) and Hölder’s inequality as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\big\|(\mathrm{I}-\mathbf{P}_{n})\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t))f(t)\big\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathrm{I}-\mathbf{P}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{H})}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t))f(t)\big\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathrm{I}-\mathbf{P}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{H})}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathrm{I}-\mathbf{P}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{H})}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t)\big)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2})\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.35)

\displaystyle\leq(1+K)\|\mathrm{I}-\mathbf{P}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{H})}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Substituting (
4.1
)-(
4.1
) in (
4.1
), we obtain the required result (
4.30
) by passing
n\to\infty
and the arbitrariness of
\delta
.

Our final aim of this Claim is to prove the following:

(4.36)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\big\|\big(\gamma_{n}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t),z)-\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t),z)\big)\big(g(t,z)-1\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t=0.

We consider

\displaystyle\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\big\|\big(\mathbf{P}_{n}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t),z)-\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t),z)\big)\big(g(t,z)-1\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\big\|\mathbf{P}_{n}\big(\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t),z)-\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t),z)\big)\big(g(t,z)-1\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

(4.37)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\big\|(\mathrm{I}-\mathbf{P}_{n})\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t),z)\big(g(t,z)-1\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t.

Using Hypothesis
3.4
(H.9) and the definition of
A_{\delta,n}
, we estimate the first term of the right hand side of the above inequality (
4.1
) as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\big\|\mathbf{P}_{n}\big(\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t),z)-\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t),z)\big)\big(g(t,z)-1\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t),z)-\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|g(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|g(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

(4.38)

\displaystyle\leq\delta\int_{A_{\delta,n}^{c}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t+2K\int_{A_{\delta,n}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t.

To estimate the final term in the right hand side of (
4.1
), we use Hypothesis
3.4
(H.8) and Hölder’s inequality as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\big\|(\mathrm{I}-\mathbf{P}_{n})\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t),z)\big(g(t,z)-1\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathrm{I}-\mathbf{P}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{H})}\int_{0}^{T}\|\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{n}(t),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|g(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

(4.39)

\displaystyle\leq(1+K)\|\mathrm{I}-\mathbf{P}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{H})}\int_{0}^{T}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t.

Substituting (
4.1
) and (
4.1
) in (
4.1
), passing
n\to\infty
and applying Lemma
3.3
(3), we obtain the required result (
4.36
). Due to the uniqueness of limits, we find
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}(\cdot)f(\cdot)=\mathrm{B}(\cdot,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(\cdot))f(\cdot)
and
\widehat{\gamma}(\cdot,z)\big(g(\cdot,z)-1\big)=\gamma(\cdot,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(\cdot),z)\big(g(\cdot,z)-1\big)
in
\mathrm{L}^{1}(0,T;\mathbb{H})
.

Using Claims 1, 2 and 3, we can pass the limit in (
4.14
) to obtain the following system for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
:

(4.40)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} \langle\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t),\boldsymbol{\phi}\rangle&=-\langle\mathfrak{C}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)),\boldsymbol{\phi}\rangle,\\
\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(0),\boldsymbol{\phi}\big)&=\big(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\phi}\big),\end{aligned}}}\right.

for all
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathbb{V}
.
Moreover, we have the following energy equality:

(4.41)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}=\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}-2\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\rangle\mathrm{d}s,

for all
t\in[0,T]
.
Therefore, the equation (
4.7
) has a weak solution
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{H})\cap\mathrm{L}^{\beta}(0,T;\mathbb{V})
, for
\beta\in(1,\infty)
, such that the following energy equality satisfied:

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+2\int_{0}^{t}\bigg\{\langle\mathrm{A}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\rangle+\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s))f(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\big)

(4.42)

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s),z)\big(g(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\bigg)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s,

for all
t\in[0,T]
.

Step 4:

Uniqueness of weak solution.
Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}(\cdot)
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}(\cdot)
be any two weak solution of (
4.7
) with the same initial data
\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{H}
. From (
4.7
), for all
t\in[0,T],
we have

\displaystyle\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}(t)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}(t),\boldsymbol{\phi}\big)=-\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}(s))-\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}(s)),\boldsymbol{\phi}\rangle\mathrm{d}s,\ \text{ for all }\ \boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathbb{V}.

We know that the energy equality (
4.41
) holds, and using the local monotoncity condition (
4.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}(t)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-2\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}(s))-\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}(s)),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}(s)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}(s)\rangle\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\bigg\{\big(\mathfrak{a}(s)+\rho(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}(s))+\eta(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}(s))\big)+2\|f(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

(4.43)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(s,z)\big|g(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s,

for all
t\in[0,T]
. Using the fact that
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{H})\cap\mathrm{L}^{\beta}(0,T;\mathbb{V})
for
\beta\in(1,\infty)
,
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathrm{L}^{1}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{+})
,
f\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{H})
and Lemma
3.1
, the uniqueness is straightforward by an application of Gronwall’s inequality in (
4.1
).
∎

Let us define

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}^{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}f(s)\mathrm{d}s,\nu_{T}^{g}}}\right)=\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}},\ \text{ for }\ \boldsymbol{p}=(f,g)\in\bar{S}^{\Upsilon},

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(\cdot)
is the unique weak solution of (
4.1
) obtained in Theorem
4.1
. Now, we are in a position to verify Condition
2.1
(i).

Proposition 4.2
.

Fix
\Upsilon\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\boldsymbol{p}_{n}=(f_{n},g_{n}),\ \boldsymbol{p}=(f,g)\in\bar{S}^{\Upsilon}
be such that
\boldsymbol{p}_{n}\to\boldsymbol{p}
as
n\to\infty
. Then we have

(4.44)

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}^{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}f_{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s,\nu_{T}^{g_{n}}}}\right)\to\mathscr{G}^{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}f(s)\mathrm{d}s,\nu_{T}^{g}}}\right)\ \text{ as }\ n\to\infty,

in
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{H})
.

Proof.

Let us fix
\mathscr{G}^{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}f_{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s,\nu_{T}^{g_{n}}}}\right):=\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}
. Then,
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(\cdot)
satisfies the following deterministic control problem:

(4.45)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathrm{d}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)&=\bigg[\mathrm{A}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t))+\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t))f_{n}(t)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t),z)\big(g_{n}(t,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg]\mathrm{d}t,\\
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(0)&=\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{H}.\end{aligned}}}\right.

Since
\boldsymbol{p}_{n}\in\bar{S}^{\Upsilon}
, using the energy estimate (
4.3
) and the Banach-Alaoglu theorem, we obtain the following convergence along a subsequence (which is denoted by the same)

(4.46)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} \widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}&\xrightharpoonup{\ast}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}},&&\text{ in }\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{H}),\\
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}&\xrightharpoonup{}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}},&&\text{ in }\mathrm{L}^{\beta}(0,T;\mathbb{V}),\\
\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}&\xrightharpoonup{}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}},&&\text{ in }\mathrm{L}^{1}(0,T;\mathbb{H})+\mathrm{L}^{\frac{\beta}{\beta-1}}(0,T;\mathbb{V}),\ \text{ for }\ \beta\in(1,\infty),\end{aligned}}}\right.

as
n\to\infty
. Using the Aubin-Lions compactness result, we obtain the strong convergence along a subsequence (still denoted by the same)

(4.47)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}\to\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}},\text{ in }\mathrm{L}^{\beta}(0,T;\mathbb{H}),\ \text{ for }\ \beta\in(1,\infty),

and along a further subsequence

(4.48)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\to\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}},\ \text{ for a.e. }\ t\in[0,T],

as
n\to\infty
. Since
\boldsymbol{p}=(f,g)\in\bar{S}^{\Upsilon}
, there exists a unique weak solution
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}=\mathscr{G}^{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}f(s)\mathrm{d}s,\nu_{T}^{g}}}\right)
of the problem (
4.1
).

Step 1:

To prove
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}=\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}
.

Let us choose a continuously differentiable function
\Psi
on
[0,T]
with
\Psi(T)=0
. Taking the inner product with
\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}
to the first equation in (
4.45
), and then integrating by parts, we find

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t),\Psi^{\prime}(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\big(\boldsymbol{x},\Psi(0)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)+\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mathrm{A}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\rangle\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\big(\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t))f_{n}(t),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)\mathrm{d}t

(4.49)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t),z)\big(g_{n}(t,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t.

From (
4.20
) and Step 3, Claim 2 in the proof of Theorem
4.1
, we easily have

(4.50)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mathrm{A}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\rangle\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mathrm{A}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\rangle\mathrm{d}t.

The convergence of the penultimate term in the right hand side of the equation (
4.1
) can be justified as follows:

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\big(\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t))f_{n}(t),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\big(\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t))f(t),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)\mathrm{d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\big((\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t))-\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)))f_{n}(t),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)\mathrm{d}t\bigg|

(4.51)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\big(\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t))(f_{n}(t)-f(t)),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)\mathrm{d}t\bigg|.

Let us take
C_{\Psi}=\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}|\Psi(t)|
. Now, we consider the first term in the right hand side of the inequality (
4.1
) and estimate it using Hypothesis
3.4
(H.7) as

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\big((\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t))-\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)))f_{n}(t),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)\mathrm{d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\Psi(t)|\int_{0}^{T}\sqrt{\mathrm{L}_{B}(t)}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|f_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Psi}\int_{0}^{T}\big(L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)+\|f_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t

(4.52)

\displaystyle\to 0\text{ as }n\to\infty,

where we have used the convergence (
4.48
), the fact that
\|f_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\to\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}
, for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
and Dominated Convergence Theorem. Note that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\big(L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)+\|f_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{T}L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)\mathrm{d}t+\Upsilon}}\right)\leq C.

The final term of the right hand side of (
4.1
) can be estimates as follows:

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\big(\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t))(f_{n}(t)-f(t)),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)\mathrm{d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Psi}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t))(f_{n}(t)-f(t))\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t

(4.53)

\displaystyle\to 0\ \text{ as }\ n\to\infty,

where we have used the fact that the operator
\mathrm{B}(\cdot,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(\cdot))
is Hilbert-Schmidt in
\mathbb{H}
and hence it is compact in
\mathbb{H}
and we know that a compact operator maps weakly convergent sequences into strongly convergent sequences. One can show the above convergence in the following way also:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t))(f_{n}(t)-f(t))\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(1+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\bigg)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(t)\mathrm{d}t}}\right)^{1/2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|f_{n}(t)-f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t}}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\to 0\ \text{ as }\ n\to\infty,

where we have used Hypothesis
1.1
(H.5) and Hölder’s inequality.

The convergence of the final term in the right hand side of the equation (
4.1
) can be justified as follows:

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t),z)\big(g_{n}(t,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t),z)\big(g(t,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\big(\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t),z)-\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t),z)\big)\big(g_{n}(t,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t\bigg|

(4.54)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t),z)\big((g_{n}(t,z)-1)-(g(t,z)-1)\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t\bigg|.

For any
\delta>0
, define
A_{\delta,n}=\{t\in[0,T]:\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}>\delta\}
. Then, using a similar argument as in (
4.31
), we conclude

(4.55)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\lambda_{T}\big(A_{\delta,n}\big)=0.

Let us fix
K=\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\vee\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}<\infty
. Now, we consider the penultimate term in the right hand side of the inequality (
4.1
) and estimate it using the fact that
g_{n}\in S^{\Upsilon}
and Hypothesis
3.4
(H.9) as

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\big(\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t),z)-\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t),z)\big)\big(g_{n}(t,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|g_{n}(t,z)-1\big||\Psi(t)|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Psi}\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|g_{n}(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

(4.56)

\displaystyle\leq\delta C_{\Psi}\int_{A_{\delta,n}^{c}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g_{n}(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t+2KC_{\Psi}\int_{A_{\delta,n}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g_{n}(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t.

Using the fact that
g_{n},g\in S^{\Upsilon}
, the definition of
A_{\delta,n}
and Hypothesis
3.4
(H.8), we estimate the final term of (
4.1
) as

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t),z)\big((g_{n}(t,z)-1)-(g(t,z)-1)\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|(g_{n}(t,z)-1)-(g(t,z)-1)\big||\Psi(t)|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Psi}\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|(g_{n}(t,z)-1)-(g(t,z)-1)\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\delta C_{\Psi}\int_{A_{\delta,n}^{c}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|(g_{n}(t,z)-1)-(g(t,z)-1)\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+KC_{\Psi}\int_{A_{\delta,n}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|(g_{n}(t,z)-1)-(g(t,z)-1)\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Psi}\bigg\{\delta\int_{A_{\delta,n}^{c}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g_{n}(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t+K\int_{A_{\delta,n}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g_{n}(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t\bigg\}

(4.57)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\Psi}\bigg\{\delta\int_{A_{\delta,n}^{c}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t+K\int_{A_{\delta,n}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t\bigg\}.

Substituting (
4.1
) and (
4.1
) in (
4.1
), passing
n\to\infty
and applying Lemma
3.3
(3), we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t),z)\big(g_{n}(t,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t

(4.58)

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t),z)\big(g(t,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t\bigg|\to 0\ \text{ as }\ n\to\infty,

since
\delta>0
is arbitrary. Combining (
4.50
)-(
4.1
) and (
4.1
) with (
4.1
), we deduce

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t),\Psi^{\prime}(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\big(\boldsymbol{x},\Psi(0)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)+\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mathrm{A}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\rangle\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\big(\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t))f(t),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)\mathrm{d}t

(4.59)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t),z)\big(g(t,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\Psi(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t.

Now, one can conclude by using similar arguments as in the proof of Theorem 3.1,
[
73
]
(see Section 3, Chapter III). Since the equality (
4.1
) holds for any linear combination of
\{\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2},\ldots\}
, therefore it is holds for any
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{V}
also by a continuity argument. By choosing
\Psi\in\mathcal{D}([0,T))
(the space of test functions over
[0,T)
), we find that
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(\cdot)
satisfies the equation (
4.1
) in the sense of distributions. Therefore, by the uniqueness of weak solutions of (
4.1
), we have
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}=\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}
.

Step 2:

To prove
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}\to\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}
in
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{H})
as
n\to\infty
.

Our aim is to establish the following convergence:

(4.60)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\to 0\ \text{ as }\ n\to\infty.

Recall that for the system (
4.1
), the energy equality (
4.1
) holds true. Define
\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(\cdot):=\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(\cdot)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(\cdot)
, so that
\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(\cdot)
satisfies the following SPDEs for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
:

(4.61)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathrm{d}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)&=\bigg[\mathrm{A}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t))-\mathrm{A}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t))+\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t))f_{n}(t)-\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t))f(t)\\
&\quad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t),z)\big(g_{n}(t,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)-\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t),z)\big(g(t,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg]\mathrm{d}t,\\
\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(0)&=\boldsymbol{0}.\end{aligned}}}\right.

It should be noted that both
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(\cdot)
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(\cdot)
satisfy the estimate (
4.3
). Taking the inner product with
\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(\cdot)
to the first equation of the system (
4.61
) and then using Hypothesis
1.1
(H.2), we get

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathrm{A}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(s))-\mathrm{A}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)),\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\rangle\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(s))f_{n}(s)-\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s))f(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\big)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\big\{\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(s),z)\big(g_{n}(s,z)-1\big)-\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s),z)\big(g(s,z)-1\big)\big\}\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\big\{\mathfrak{a}(s)+\rho(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(s))+\eta(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s))\big\}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\big((\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(s))-\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)))f_{n}(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\big)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s))\big(f_{n}(s)-f(s)\big),\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\big)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\big(\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(s),z)-\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s),z)\big)\big(g_{n}(s,z)-1\big),\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)}}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(s),z)\big((g_{n}(s,z)-1)-(g(s,z)-1)\big),\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)}}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s

(4.62)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\big\{\mathfrak{a}(s)+\rho(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(s))+\eta(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s))\big\}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\sum_{i=1}^{4}|J_{i}^{n}(t)|,

for all
t\in[0,T]
, where
J_{i}^{n}
, for
i=1,\ldots,4
represent the final four terms. Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Hypothesis
3.4
(H.7) and Young’s inequality, we estimate
|J_{1}^{n}(t)|
as

\displaystyle|J_{1}^{n}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(s))-\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\|f_{n}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{L_{\mathrm{B}}(s)}\|f_{n}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

(4.63)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\big\{L_{\mathrm{B}}(s)+\|f_{n}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big\}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s.

Now, we consider the term
|J_{3}^{n}(t)|
, and estimate it using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Hypothesis
3.4
(H.9) and Lemma
3.3
as

\displaystyle|J_{3}^{n}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(s),z)-\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|g_{n}(s,z)-1\big|\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s

(4.64)

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(s,z)\big|g_{n}(s,z)-1\big|\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s.

Substituting (
4.1
) and (
4.1
) in (
4.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\bigg\{\mathfrak{a}(s)+\rho(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(s))+\eta(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(s))+L_{\mathrm{B}}(s)+\|f_{n}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

(4.65)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(s,z)\big|g_{n}(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+|J_{2}^{n}(t)|+|J_{4}^{n}(t)|,

for all
t\in[0,T]
. An application of Gronwall’s inequality in (
4.1
) yields

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\{\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big[|J_{2}^{n}(t)|+|J_{4}^{n}(t)|\big]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\times\exp\bigg\{\int_{0}^{T}\bigg[\mathfrak{a}(t)+\rho(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t))+\eta(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t))+L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)+\|f_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

(4.66)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g_{n}(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg]\mathrm{d}t\bigg\}.

Note that the integral appearing in the exponential is independent of
n,
since
\boldsymbol{p}_{n},\boldsymbol{p}\in\bar{S}^{\Upsilon}

\rho(\cdot)
satisfies Hypothesis
1.1
(H.2) and
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(\cdot)
has the bound given in (
4.3
).

Now, for any
\delta>0
, let us define
A_{\delta,n}=\big\{t\in[0,T]:\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}>\delta\}
. Then, by using the similar calculations as in (
4.31
), we have
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\lambda_{T}(A_{\delta,n})=0
.

Splitting the interval
[0,T]
into two parts, we estimate
\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{2}^{n}(t)|
as

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{2}^{n}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{T}\sqrt{L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)}\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\|f_{n}(t)-f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2\bigg\{\int_{0}^{T}\sqrt{L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)}\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\|f_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\sqrt{L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)}\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\bigg\{C\int_{A_{\delta,n}}\sqrt{L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)}\|f_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t+\delta C\int_{A_{\delta,n}^{c}}\sqrt{L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)}\|f_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t\bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad+2\bigg\{C\int_{A_{\delta,n}}\sqrt{L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)}\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t+\delta C\int_{A_{\delta,n}^{c}}\sqrt{L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)}\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg\{\bigg(\int_{A_{\delta,n}}L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\|f_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\|f_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\bigg)

(4.67)

\displaystyle\quad+C\bigg\{\bigg(\int_{A_{\delta,n}}L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\|f(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\bigg),

where we have used the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Hypothesis
3.4
(H.7) and Young’s inequality. Using the absolute continuity of the Lebesgue integral in (
4.1
), we find

(4.68)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{2}^{n}(t)|=0.

Again, we split the interval
[0,T]
into two parts, and using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Hypothesis
3.4
(H.8) and Young’s inequality. we estimate
\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{4}^{n}(t)|
as

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{4}^{n}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|g_{n}(t,z)-1\big|\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|g(t,z)-1\big|\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\big|g_{n}(t,z)-1\big|\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\big|g(t,z)-1\big|\|\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\boldsymbol{p}_{n}}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{A_{\delta,n}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g_{n}(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t+\delta C\int_{A_{\delta,n}^{c}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g_{n}(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t

(4.69)

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{A_{\delta,n}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t+\delta C\int_{A_{\delta,n}^{c}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|g(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t.

Using Lemma
3.3
in (
4.1
), we deduce

(4.70)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{4}^{n}(t)|=0.

Substituting (
4.68
) and (
4.70
) in (
4.1
), we obtain the required result (
4.60
).
∎

5.
Verification of Condition
2.1
(2)

Let us now verify Condition (2) in
2.1
. A similar verification result for a class of SPDEs with locally monotone coefficients has been obtained in
[
76
]
. Fix
\Upsilon\in\mathbb{N}
, for any
\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}=(\psi_{\epsilon},\varphi_{\epsilon})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\Upsilon}
, we consider the following controlled SPDEs for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
in
\mathbb{V}^{*}
:

(5.1)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathrm{d}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)&=\mathrm{A}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t))\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t))\psi_{\epsilon}(t)\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{\epsilon}\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t))\mathrm{d}\mathrm{W}(t)\\
&\quad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t),z)\big(\varphi_{\epsilon}(t,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t+\epsilon\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t-),z)\widetilde{N}^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}z),\\
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(0)&=\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{H}.\end{aligned}}}\right.

Remember that
\widetilde{N}^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}=N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}-\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}\lambda_{T}\otimes\nu
, where
\lambda_{T}
is the Lebesgue measure and
\nu
is the intensity measure.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
(\bar{\mathbf{V}},\mathcal{B}(\bar{\mathbf{V}}),\{\bar{\mathscr{F}}_{t}^{\bar{\mathbf{V}}}\}_{t\geq 0},\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\bar{\mathbf{V}}})
be the filtered probability space (see subsection
2.4
). Assume the initial data
\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{H}
. Then (
5.1
) has a
probabilistically strong solution
if and only if there exists a progressively measurable process
\mathbf{Y}^{\epsilon}:[0,T]\times\bar{\mathbf{V}}\to\mathbb{H}
,
\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\bar{\mathbf{V}}}
-a.s., paths

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}^{\epsilon}(t,\omega)\in\mathrm{D}([0,T];\mathbb{H})\cap\mathrm{L}^{\beta}(0,T;\mathbb{V}),\;\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\bar{\mathbf{V}}}\text{-a.s., for }\beta\in(1,\infty),

and the following equality holds
\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\bar{\mathbf{V}}}
-a.s., in
\mathbb{V}^{*}
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}^{\epsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{x}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{A}(s,\mathbf{Y}^{\epsilon}(s))\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}^{\epsilon}(s))\mathrm{d}\mathrm{W}(s)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s),z)\big(\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s+\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}^{\epsilon}(s-),z)\widetilde{N}^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z),

for all
t\in[0,T]
.

Let us take
\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}=(\psi_{\epsilon},\varphi_{\epsilon})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\Upsilon}
and define
\nu_{\epsilon}=\frac{1}{\varphi_{\epsilon}}
. Then, we have the following result (for proof see Theorem III.3.24,
[
36
]
, Lemma 2.3,
[
19
]
):

Lemma 5.2
.

The processes

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{t}^{\epsilon}(\nu_{\epsilon})

\displaystyle:=\exp\bigg\{\int_{(0,t]\times\mathrm{Z}\times[0,\epsilon^{-1}]}\ln\big(\nu_{\epsilon}(s,z)\big)\bar{N}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z,\mathrm{d}r)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{(0,t]\times\mathrm{Z}\times[0,\epsilon^{-1}]}\big(-\nu_{\epsilon}(s,z)+1\big)\bar{\nu}_{T}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z,\mathrm{d}r)\bigg\},

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{\epsilon}(\psi_{\epsilon})

\displaystyle:=\exp\bigg\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\int_{0}^{t}\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\mathrm{d}\mathrm{W}(s)-\frac{1}{2\epsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\|\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg\},

are
\{\bar{\mathscr{F}}_{t}^{\bar{\mathbf{V}}}\}
-martingales. Set

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{\epsilon}(\psi_{\epsilon},\nu_{\epsilon}):=\mathcal{E}_{t}^{\epsilon}(\nu_{\epsilon})\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{\epsilon}(\psi_{\epsilon}).

Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{t}^{\epsilon}(\mathrm{G})=\int_{\mathrm{G}}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{\epsilon}(\psi_{\epsilon},\nu_{\epsilon})\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\bar{\mathbf{V}}},\ \text{ for }\ \mathrm{G}\in\mathcal{B}(\bar{\mathbf{V}}),

defines a probability measure in
\bar{\mathbf{V}}
.

Define the process
\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{Q}^{\epsilon}_{t}}(\cdot)
and the random measure
\widetilde{N}_{\mathbb{Q}^{\epsilon}_{t}}^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\cdot,\cdot)
by
\mathrm{d}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{Q}^{\epsilon}_{t}}(t):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\psi_{\epsilon}(t)\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}\mathrm{W}(t)
and
\widetilde{N}_{\mathbb{Q}^{\epsilon}_{t}}^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}z):=\epsilon^{-1}(\phi_{\epsilon}(t,z)-1)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}t+\widetilde{N}^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)
, respectively. Then an application of Girsanov’s theorem (see Theorem 1.29,
[
63
]
) yields
\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{Q}^{\epsilon}_{t}}(\cdot)
is a Brownian motion with respect to
\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{\bar{\mathbf{V}}}\}_{t\geq 0}
and
\mathbb{Q}^{\epsilon}_{t}
and
\widetilde{N}_{\mathbb{Q}^{\epsilon}_{t}}^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\cdot,\cdot)
is the
(\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{\bar{\mathbf{V}}}\}_{t\geq 0},\mathbb{Q}^{\epsilon}_{t})
-compensated Poisson random measure of
{N}_{\mathbb{Q}^{\epsilon}_{t}}^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\cdot,\cdot)
. Under the above change in measures, one can transform the system (
5.1
) into the following form:

(5.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t

\displaystyle)=\mathrm{A}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t))\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{\epsilon}\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t))\mathrm{d}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{Q}^{\epsilon}_{t}}(t)+\epsilon\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t-),z)\widetilde{N}_{\mathbb{Q}^{\epsilon}_{t}}^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}z),

for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
in
\mathbb{V}^{*}
. The system (
5.2
) is similar to the one given in (
1.1
) whose existence of a unique pathwise strong solution is known from Theorem
1.4
. With the above settings, we have the following result on the solution representation:

Lemma 5.3

(Lemma 7.1,
[
16
]
)
.

Assume
\epsilon>0
, for every process
\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}=(\psi_{\epsilon},\varphi_{\epsilon})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\Upsilon}
defined on the the probability space
(\bar{\mathbf{V}},\mathcal{B}(\bar{\mathbf{V}}),\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{\bar{\mathbf{V}}}\},\mathbb{Q}_{T}^{\epsilon})
, the process
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}
defined by

(5.3)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}=\mathscr{G}^{\epsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\epsilon}\mathrm{W}(\cdot)+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\mathrm{d}s,\epsilon N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}}}\right),

is the unique strong solution of the controlled SPDEs (
5.1
).

5.1.
Tightness of the family
\{\mathscr{L}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon})\}

Let us now establish the tightness of the family
\{\mathscr{L}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon})\}_{\epsilon>0}
. The following estimates (Lemmas
5.4
and
5.5
) are helpful in the sequel.

Lemma 5.4
.

For
p=2,2+\alpha
or
M
in the Hypothesis
3.4
(H.8) and
\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}=(\psi_{\epsilon},\varphi_{\epsilon})\in{\mathcal{U}}^{\Upsilon}
there exists
\epsilon_{p},C_{p}>0
, such that

(5.4)

\displaystyle\sup_{\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{p}]}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}}}\right)+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\mathrm{d}t}}\right)\leq C_{p}(1+\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}).

Proof.

Applying infinite dimensional Itô’s formula (see Theorem 1.2,
[
32
]
) to the process
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(\cdot)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}
, we find for all
t\in[0,T]

(5.5)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}=\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}+\sum_{j=1}^{7}J_{j}(t),

where

\displaystyle J_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\big[2\langle\mathrm{A}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\rangle+\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}^{2}\big]\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle J_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{p(p-2)}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-4}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\circ\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle J_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\epsilon}p\int_{0}^{t}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\mathrm{d}\mathrm{W}(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\big),

\displaystyle J_{4}(t)

\displaystyle=p\int_{0}^{t}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\psi_{\epsilon}(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\big),

\displaystyle J_{5}(t)

\displaystyle=p\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\big(\epsilon\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\big)\widetilde{N}^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z),

\displaystyle J_{6}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)+\epsilon\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}-\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}

\displaystyle\qquad-p\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\big(\epsilon\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\big)\big]N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z),

and

\displaystyle J_{7}(t)

\displaystyle=p\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\big(\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s),z)(\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s.

We consider the term
J_{1}(\cdot)
and estimate it using Hypothesis
1.1
(H.3), (H.5), Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities as

\displaystyle|J_{1}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\bigg\{2\big[\mathfrak{a}(s)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)-L_{\mathrm{A}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\big]+\mathfrak{b}(s)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq-pL_{\mathrm{A}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\mathrm{d}s+C\int_{0}^{t}\big[\mathfrak{a}(s)+\mathfrak{b}(s)\big]\mathrm{d}s

(5.6)

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{0}^{t}\big[\mathfrak{a}(s)+\mathfrak{b}(s)\big]\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\mathrm{d}s.

We estimate the term
J_{2}(\cdot)
using Hypothesis
1.1
(H.5), Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities as

\displaystyle J_{2}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p(p-2)}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\big\{\mathfrak{b}(s)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\big\}\mathrm{d}s

(5.7)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{b}(s)\mathrm{d}s+C\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{b}(s)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\mathrm{d}s.

Now, we consider the term
J_{4}(\cdot)
and estimate it using Hypothesis
1.1
(H.5) and Young’s inequality as

\displaystyle|J_{4}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{0}^{t}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-1}\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\|\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{0}^{t}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-1}\|\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big[\mathfrak{b}(s)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{\mathfrak{b}(s)}\big(1+2\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\big)\|\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}s

(5.8)

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{b}(s)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\|\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\big[\mathfrak{b}(s)+\|\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big]\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\mathrm{d}s\bigg).

For the term
J_{6}(\cdot)
, we apply the following inequality

\displaystyle\big|\|\boldsymbol{x}+\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}-\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}-p\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{h})\big|\leq C_{p}\big(\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big),

to find

(5.9)

\displaystyle|J_{6}(t)|\leq C_{p}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\big(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\|\epsilon\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\|\epsilon\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\big)N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z).

Using Hypothesis
3.4
(H.8), we find

\displaystyle|J_{7}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-1}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}|\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-1}\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)L_{\gamma}(s,z)|\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(s,z)|\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s

(5.10)

\displaystyle\quad+2p\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}L_{\gamma}(s,z)|\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s.

Substituting (
5.1
)-(
5.1
) in (
5.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}+pL_{\mathrm{A}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}+C\int_{0}^{t}\big[\mathfrak{a}(s)+\mathfrak{b}(s)+\|f(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big]\mathrm{d}s+C\int_{0}^{t}\big[\mathfrak{a}(s)+\mathfrak{b}(s)+\|f(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big]\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\big(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\|\epsilon\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\|\epsilon\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\big)N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle\quad+p\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(s,z)|\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s+2p\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}L_{\gamma}(s,z)|\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s

(5.11)

\displaystyle\quad+|J_{3}(t)|+|J_{4}(t)|,

for all
t\in[0,T]
. An application of Gronwall’s inequality in (
5.1
) yields

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}+pL_{\mathrm{A}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}+C\int_{0}^{T}\big[\mathfrak{a}(s)+\mathfrak{b}(s)\big]\mathrm{d}s+C\Upsilon+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{3}(t)|+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{4}(t)|+pC_{L_{\gamma},\Upsilon}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\big(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\|\epsilon\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\|\epsilon\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\big)N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)\bigg)

(5.12)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp\bigg(C\int_{0}^{T}\big[\mathfrak{a}(s)+\mathfrak{b}(s)\big]\mathrm{d}s+C\Upsilon+2pC_{L_{\gamma},\Upsilon}\bigg),

where we have used Lemma
3.1
and the fact that
\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}=(\psi_{\epsilon},\varphi_{\epsilon})\in{\mathcal{U}}^{\Upsilon}
. Now, we consider the term
\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{3}(t)|
and estimate it using Hypothesis
1.1
(H.5), the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy, Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities as

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|J_{3}(s)|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\epsilon}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|^{2p-2}\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\epsilon}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\bigg]+C_{p}\epsilon\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\mathfrak{b}(s)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\bigg]+\epsilon\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]+C_{{\epsilon},p}\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\mathrm{d}s

(5.13)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{1}{4}+\epsilon\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\bigg]+C_{{\epsilon},p}\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\mathrm{d}s.

Once again using the Hypothesis
3.4
(H.8), Lemma
3.1
, the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy, Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, we find

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|J_{5}(t)|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p-4}|\big(\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\big)|^{2}N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p-2}L_{\gamma}^{2}(s,z)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)^{2}N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\bigg(\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\bigg)\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}L_{\gamma}^{2}(s,z)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)^{2}N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\bigg]+C_{p}\epsilon\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\bigg(1+\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\bigg)\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}^{2}(s,z)\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

(5.14)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{1}{4}+\epsilon C_{p}C_{L_{\gamma},2,2,\Upsilon}\bigg)\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\bigg]+\epsilon C_{p}C_{L_{\gamma},2,2,\Upsilon}.

Again, using Hypothesis
3.4
(H.8), Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
3.1
, we find

\displaystyle C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\|\epsilon\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}L_{\gamma}^{2}(s,z)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)^{2}\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\bigg(1+\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\bigg)\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}^{2}(s,z)\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

(5.15)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C_{p}C_{L_{\gamma},2,2,\Upsilon}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\bigg]+\epsilon C_{p}C_{L_{\gamma},2,2,\Upsilon},

and

\displaystyle C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\epsilon\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{p-1}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{p-1}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\bigg(\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}+1\bigg)\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}^{p}(s,z)\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

(5.16)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{p-1}C_{p}C_{L_{\gamma},p,p,\Upsilon}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\bigg]+\epsilon^{p-1}C_{p}C_{L_{\gamma},p,p,\Upsilon}.

Finally, combining (
5.1
)-(
5.1
), we obtain that there exists a positive
\epsilon_{p}
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{p}]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\bigg]+pL_{\mathrm{A}}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]}}\right\}

(5.17)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Upsilon,p,T,\int_{0}^{T}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathfrak{a}(s)+\mathfrak{b}(s)}}\right]\mathrm{d}s,L_{\gamma}}(1+\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}),

so that the proof can be completed.
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

For
p=\frac{M}{2}
, there exists a constant
C_{p}
such that

(5.18)

\displaystyle\sup_{\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{2p})}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\mathrm{d}s\bigg)^{p}\bigg]\leq C_{p},

where
\epsilon_{2p}
is the constant coming from Lemma
5.4
.

Proof.

Applying Itô’s formula to the process
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(\cdot)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big\{2\langle\mathrm{A}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\rangle+\epsilon\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}^{2}\big\}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\sqrt{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\mathrm{d}\mathrm{W}(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\big)+\int_{0}^{t}\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\psi_{\epsilon}(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\big)

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\big(\epsilon\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-)\big)\widetilde{N}^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\epsilon\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\big(\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s),z)(\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s

(5.19)

\displaystyle=:\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}I_{i}(t).

We consider the term
I_{1}(\cdot)
and estimate it using the Hypothesis
1.1
(H.3) and (H.5) as

(5.20)

\displaystyle I_{1}(t)\leq\int_{0}^{t}\big\{2\mathfrak{a}(s)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)-2L_{\mathrm{A}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}+\epsilon\mathfrak{b}(s)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\big\}\mathrm{d}s.

For rest of the calculations, we fix
p=M/2
. Now, we consider
\bar{\mathbb{E}}\big[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}|I_{2}(t)|^{p}\big]
and estimate it using the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (see Theorem 1.1,
[
21
]
), Hypothesis
1.1
(H.5), Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities as

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|I_{2}(t)|^{p}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{\frac{p}{2}}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{\frac{p}{2}}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p}\bigg]+C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{p}

(5.21)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{\frac{p}{2}}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p}\bigg]+C_{p}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)^{p}\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p}\bigg]\bigg)

Let us consider the term
\bar{\mathbb{E}}\big[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}|I_{3}(t)|^{p}\big]
and estimate it using Hypothesis
1.1
(H.5), the Cauchy-Schwarz, Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities as

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|I_{3}(t)|^{p}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\|\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\sqrt{\mathfrak{b}(s)}\big(1+2\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\|\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{p}

(5.22)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p}\bigg]}}\right)\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\mathrm{d}s+\Upsilon\bigg]^{p}.

With the help of Corollary 2.4,
[
14
]
, Hypothesis (H.8), Lemmas
3.1
and
5.4
, we estimate the term
\bar{\mathbb{E}}\big[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}|I_{5}(t)|^{p}\big]
as

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}|I_{5}(t)|^{p}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{2p}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)\bigg)^{p}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{2p-1}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p}\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon^{p}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{2p-1}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}^{2p}(s,z)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p}\big)\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon^{p}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}^{2}(s,z)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{2p-1}C_{p}\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg(\sup_{\varphi\in S^{\Upsilon}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}^{2p}(s,z)\varphi(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon^{p}C_{p}\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg(\sup_{\varphi\in S^{\Upsilon}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}^{2}(s,z)\varphi(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)^{p}

(5.23)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\bigg(\epsilon^{2p-1}C_{L_{\gamma},2p,2p,\Upsilon}+\epsilon^{p}C_{L_{\gamma},2,2,\Upsilon}^{p}\bigg)\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p}\bigg]\bigg).

Now, we consider the term
\bar{\mathbb{E}}\big[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}|I_{4}(t)|^{p}\big]
and estimate it using the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (see Theorem 1.1,
[
21
]
) and (
5.1
) as

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|I_{4}(t)|^{p}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{p}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)\bigg]^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{p}C_{p}\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\bigg]+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)\bigg]^{p}\bigg\}

(5.24)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{p}C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{p}\bigg]+C_{p}\bigg(\epsilon^{p-1}C_{L_{\gamma},2p,2p,\Upsilon}+C_{L_{\gamma},2,2,\Upsilon}^{p}\bigg)\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p}\bigg]\bigg).

Using Hypothesis
3.4
(H.8), Lemmas
3.1
and
5.4
, we estimate the term
\bar{\mathbb{E}}\big[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}|I_{6}(t)|^{p}\big]
as

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|I_{6}(t)|^{p}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}|\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}(s,z)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)|\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg(\sup_{\varphi\in S^{\Upsilon}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}(s,z)|\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)^{p}

(5.25)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}C_{L_{\gamma},\Upsilon}^{p}\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2p}\bigg]\bigg).

Substituting (
5.20
)-(
5.1
) in (
5.1
) and using Lemmas
3.1
and
5.4
, we obtain the required estimate (
5.18
).
∎

Let us move to our main result of this subsection, that is, the tightness of the laws of
\{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}\}_{\epsilon>0}
. A similar result is established in
[
76
]
(see Proposition 4.1), where the authors consider SPDEs with locally monotone coefficients perturbed by pure jump noise.

Proposition 5.6
.

For some
\epsilon>0
, the family
\{\mathscr{L}(\mathbf{Y}^{\epsilon})\}_{\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0}]}
is tight in the space
\mathrm{D}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
with the Skorokhod topology. Furthermore, set

\displaystyle X_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{A}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle X_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle X_{3}^{\epsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s),z)\big(\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle M_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\mathrm{d}\mathrm{W}(s),

\displaystyle M_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s-),z)\widetilde{N}^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z).

Then

(1)

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\|M_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]=\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\|M_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]=0
,

(2)

\{X_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)\}_{t\in[0,T]}
is tight in
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
,

(3)

\{X_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)\}_{t\in[0,T]}
is tight in
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
,

(4)

\{X_{3}^{\epsilon}(t)\}_{t\in[0,T]}
is tight in
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
.

Proof.

The proof is divided into the following steps:

Step 1:

Proof of (1).

First, we prove the statement (1). Using the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (see Theorem 1.1,
[
54
]
), Hypothesis
1.1
(H.5), Lemmas
3.1
and
5.4
, we deduce

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|M_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq{\epsilon}C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq{\epsilon}C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq{\epsilon}C\bigg\{\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\bigg]\bigg\}

(5.26)

\displaystyle\to 0\ \text{ as }\ \epsilon\to 0.

Now, we consider the term
\bar{\mathbb{E}}\big[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\|M_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big]
and estimate it using the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (see Theorem 1.1,
[
54
]
), Hypothesis
1.1
(H.8) and Lemma
5.4
as

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|M_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}^{2}(s,z)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)^{2}\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg(\sup_{\varphi\in S^{\Upsilon}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}^{2}(s,z)\varphi(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\bigg)2C_{L_{\gamma},2,2,\Upsilon}

(5.27)

\displaystyle\to 0\ \text{ as }\ \epsilon\to 0.

In order to prove the tightness property, it is enough to show that for any
\delta>0
, there exists a compact subset
K_{\delta}\subset{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
, such that

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{P}}(X_{i}^{\epsilon}\in K_{\delta})>1-\delta,\ \text{ for }\ i=1,2,3.

Step 2:

Proof of (3) and (4).

Let us define

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon}

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{(\boldsymbol{r}(t),\boldsymbol{q}(t)):\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathrm{D}([0,T];\mathbb{H})\cap\mathrm{L}^{\beta}(0,T;\mathbb{V}),\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\boldsymbol{r}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\leq N,\boldsymbol{q}\in\mathcal{U}^{\Upsilon}\bigg\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon})

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{h_{1}(\cdot)=\int_{0}^{\cdot}\mathrm{B}(s,\boldsymbol{r}(s))\psi(s)\mathrm{d}s,\;(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{q})\in\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon}\bigg\}.

For any
h_{1}\in\mathcal{R}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon})
, by using Hypothesis
1.1
(H.5), Cauchy-Schwarz and Hölder’s inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\|h_{1}(t)-h_{1}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{s}^{t}\|\mathrm{B}(\tau,\boldsymbol{r}(\tau))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\|\psi(\tau)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tau\in[s,t]}\big(1+\|\boldsymbol{r}(\tau)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\int_{s}^{t}\sqrt{\mathfrak{b}(\tau)}\|\psi(\tau)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq(1+N)\bigg(\int_{s}^{t}\mathfrak{b}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int_{s}^{t}\|\psi(\tau)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(5.28)

\displaystyle\leq(1+N)\Upsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int_{s}^{t}\mathfrak{b}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Now, using the fact that
\mathfrak{b}\in\mathrm{L}^{1}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{+})
, we conclude:

•

for any
\xi>0
, there exists a
\varpi>0
independent of
h
such that for any
s,t\in[0,T]
and
|t-s|\leq\varpi
, we have

\displaystyle\|h_{1}(t)-h_{1}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\leq\xi,\ \text{ for all }\ h\in\mathcal{R}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon}),

•

and

\displaystyle\sup_{h_{1}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon})}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|h_{1}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}=\sup_{h_{1}\in\mathcal{R}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon})}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|h_{1}(t)-h_{1}(0)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\leq(1+N)\Upsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(t)\mathrm{d}t}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Since, the embedding
\mathbb{V}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}
is compact and hence
\mathbb{H}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{V}^{*}
also. By the Arzelá-Ascoli theorem (see Appendix A5,
[
67
]
),
\mathcal{R}_{1}^{c}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon})
(complement of
\mathcal{R}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon})
) in
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
, is a compact subset of
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
.

Therefore, an application of Markov’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{P}}\big(X_{2}^{\epsilon}\in\mathcal{R}_{1}^{c}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon})\big)

\displaystyle\geq\bar{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\leq N\bigg)=1-\bar{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}>N\bigg)

(5.29)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\geq 1-\frac{C}{N^{2}},

where we have used Lemma
5.4
. Hence, we obtain the tightness of
\{X_{2}^{\epsilon}\}
in
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
.

Let us now define

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon})

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{h_{2}(\cdot)=\int_{0}^{\cdot}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\boldsymbol{r}(s),z)\big(\varphi(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s,\ (\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{q})\in\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon}\bigg\}.

For any
h_{2}\in\mathcal{R}_{2}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon})
, we have

\displaystyle\|h_{2}(t)-h_{2}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\gamma(\tau,\boldsymbol{r}(\tau),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}|\varphi(\tau,z)-1|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tau\in[s,t]}\big(1+\|\boldsymbol{r}(\tau)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(\tau,z)|\varphi(\tau,z)-1|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}\tau

(5.30)

\displaystyle\leq(1+N)\sup_{\varphi\in S^{\Upsilon}}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(\tau,z)|\varphi(\tau,z)-1|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}\tau.

Using Lemma
3.1
in the above inequality, we deduce:

•

for any positive
\xi
, there exists a positive
\varpi
independent of
h_{2}
such that for any
s,t\in[0,T]
and
|t-s|\leq\varpi
, we have

\displaystyle\|h_{2}(t)-h_{2}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\leq\xi,\ \text{ for all }\ h_{2}\in\mathcal{R}_{2}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon}),

•

and

\displaystyle\sup_{h_{2}\in\mathcal{R}_{2}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon})}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|h_{2}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}=\sup_{h\in\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon})}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|h_{2}(t)-h_{2}(0)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\leq(1+N)C_{L_{\gamma},\Upsilon}.

Once again an application of Arzelá-Ascoli theorem yields
\mathcal{R}_{2}^{c}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon})
(complement of
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{D}_{N,\Upsilon})
) in
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
, is a compact subset of
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
.
A calculation similar to (
5.1
) provides the tightness of
\{X_{3}^{\epsilon}\}
in
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
.

Step 3:

Proof of (2).

Let us now move to the proof of part (2). Using Hölder’s, Young’s inequalities, Hypothesis
1.1
(H.4), Lemmas
3.1
and
5.4
, recall
\eta_{0}
in Hypothesis
3.4
(H.8), for
p=\beta+\eta_{0}
, we find

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|X_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)-X_{1}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{s}^{t}\|\mathrm{A}(\tau,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(\tau))\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg]^{p}

\displaystyle\leq|t-s|^{\frac{p}{\beta}}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{s}^{t}\|\mathrm{A}(\tau,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(\tau))\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}^{\frac{\beta}{\beta-1}}\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg]^{\frac{(\beta-1)p}{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq|t-s|^{\frac{p}{\beta}}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{s}^{t}\big(\mathfrak{a}(\tau)+C\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(\tau)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\big)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(\tau)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{\alpha}\big)\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg]^{\frac{(\beta-1)p}{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq C|t-s|^{\frac{p}{\beta}}\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{s}^{t}\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(\tau)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{\alpha}\big)\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg]^{\frac{2p(\beta-1)}{\beta}}+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{s}^{t}\big(\mathfrak{a}(\tau)+C\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(\tau)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\big)\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg]^{\frac{2p(\beta-1)}{\beta}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p,\beta,\mathfrak{a}}|t-s|^{\frac{p}{\beta}}.

Using Kolmogorov’s criterion (cf. Theorem 3.3,
[
27
]
), for every
\varpi\in\big(0,\frac{1}{\beta}-\frac{1}{p}\big)
, there exists a constant
C_{\varpi}
(independent of
\epsilon
) such that

(5.31)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\neq s\in[0,T]}\frac{\|X_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)-X_{1}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}^{p}}{|t-s|^{p\varpi}}\bigg]\leq C_{\varpi}.

From (
5.1
), we have for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
in
\mathbb{V}^{*}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)=\boldsymbol{x}+X_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)+X_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)+X_{3}^{\epsilon}(t)+M_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)+M_{2}^{\epsilon}(t).

Then

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg\{\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]

(5.32)

\displaystyle\quad+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X_{3}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|M_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|M_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\bigg\}.

One should note that

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s))\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\sqrt{\mathfrak{b}(s)}\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\|\psi_{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{2}

(5.33)

\displaystyle\leq 2\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\mathrm{d}s+\Upsilon\bigg)^{2},

and

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X_{3}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|\varphi_{\epsilon}(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{2}
