\displaystyle\leq 2\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg(\sup_{\varphi\in S^{\Upsilon}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}(s,z)\big|\varphi(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)^{2}

(5.34)

\displaystyle\leq C_{L_{\gamma},\Upsilon}\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\bigg).

Combining (
5.1
), (
5.1
), (
5.1
), (
5.1
) and Lemma
5.4
, we find

(5.35)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|X_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\leq C<\infty,

where a constant
C
which is independent of
\epsilon
.

For
\varpi\in(0,1)
and
R>0
, define

\displaystyle K_{\varpi,R}:=\bigg\{\boldsymbol{y}\in{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*}):\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\boldsymbol{y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}+\sup_{t\neq s\in[0,T]}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{y}(t)-\boldsymbol{y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}}{|t-s|^{\varpi}}\leq R\bigg\}.

Once again using the Arzelá-Ascoli theorem, one can see that
K_{\varpi,R}
is a compact subset of
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
. Using (
5.31
), (
5.35
) and Markov’s inequality, for some
\varpi\in(0,1)
and any
R>0
, we find

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{P}}(X_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)\notin K_{\varpi,R})\geq\frac{C_{\varpi,T}}{R}.

Thus, we obtain the tightness of
\{X_{1}^{\epsilon}\}
in the space
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
.

Finally, the tightness of laws of
\{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}\}_{\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0}]}
in the space
\mathrm{D}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
follows from the above conclusions.
∎

5.2.
Convergence of the processes

The aim of this section is to identify the limit function of the sequence
\{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}\}_{\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0}]}
as a solution of the equation (
5.12
) given below. We will use the tightness property which has been established in the previous section. We have already proved that the limits of
M_{1}^{\epsilon}
and
M_{2}^{\epsilon}
goes to 0 as
\epsilon\to 0
in Proposition
5.6
, so we only need to take care of the remaining three terms. Let us denote the limit function of
\{\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}\}_{\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0}]}
by
\mathbf{Y}
. In this section, we assume that for a.e.
\omega
,
\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}=(\psi_{\epsilon},\varphi_{\epsilon})(\omega)\in\bar{S}^{\Upsilon}
converges to
\boldsymbol{q}=(\psi,\varphi)(\omega)
in
\bar{S}^{\Upsilon}
weakly and
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(\omega)
converges to
\mathbf{Y}({\omega})
in
\mathrm{D}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
strongly with respect to the supremum norm. A similar result to the next Lemma has been obtained in
[
76
]
(Lemma 5.1).

Lemma 5.7
.

There exists a subsequence
\{\epsilon_{j}\}
,
\bar{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathrm{L}^{\beta}(\Omega\times[0,T];\mathbb{V})\cap\mathrm{L}^{\alpha+2}(\Omega;\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{H}))
and
\widehat{\mathrm{A}}(\cdot)\in\mathrm{L}^{\frac{\beta}{\beta-1}}(\Omega\times[0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
, for
\beta\in(1,\infty)
such that

(1)

\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}\xrightharpoonup{}\bar{\mathbf{Y}}
in
\mathrm{L}^{\beta}(\Omega\times[0,T];\mathbb{V})
, for
\beta\in(1,\infty)
;

(2)

\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}\xrightharpoonup{\ast}\bar{\mathbf{Y}}
in
\mathrm{L}^{p}(\Omega;\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{H}))
;

(3)

\mathrm{A}(\cdot,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}})\xrightharpoonup{}\widehat{\mathrm{A}}(\cdot)
in
\mathrm{L}^{\frac{\beta}{\beta-1}}(\Omega\times[0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
;

(4)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}\bigg]=0,

and for
m=\frac{\beta}{\beta+1}
,

(5.36)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2m}\mathrm{d}t\bigg]=0.

Proof.

Using the uniform estimate (
5.4
), an application of the Banach-Alaoglu theorem implies (1) and (2).

Using the Hypothesis
1.1
(H.4), Hölder’s inequality, Lemmas
5.4
and
5.5
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{A}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t))\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}^{\frac{\beta}{\beta-1}}\mathrm{d}t\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\big(\mathfrak{a}(t)+C\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\big)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{\alpha}\big)\mathrm{d}t\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[1+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{\alpha}\bigg]^{\frac{\alpha+2}{\alpha}}\bigg\}^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+2}}\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\big(\mathfrak{a}(t)+C\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\big)\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{\alpha+2}{2}}\bigg\}^{\frac{2}{\alpha+2}}

(5.37)

\displaystyle\leq C<\infty.

By Lemma
5.4
, we have

(5.38)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\leq C,\ \text{ and }\ \bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\mathrm{d}t\bigg]\leq C.

Hence, by the strong convergence of
\mathbf{Y}^{\epsilon}(\omega)
to
\mathbf{Y}(\omega)
in the space
\mathrm{D}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
with respect to the supremum norm, Fatou’s lemma and (
5.38
), we have

(5.39)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\leq C,

(5.40)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\mathrm{d}t\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\mathrm{d}t\bigg]\leq C,

and

(5.41)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}\bigg]=0.

The above convergence (
5.41
) can be justified as follows:

Define

\displaystyle\Omega_{\delta}^{\epsilon}:=\bigg\{\omega:\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}\geq\delta\bigg\}.

The strong convergence of
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(\omega)
to
\mathbf{Y}(\omega)
in the space
\mathrm{D}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
with respect to supremum norm implies

(5.42)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{\delta}^{\epsilon})=0,\text{ for all }\delta>0.

Using (
5.38
)-(
5.42
), we deduce

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}\cdot\chi_{\Omega_{\delta}^{\epsilon}}\bigg]+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}\cdot\chi_{({\Omega_{\delta}^{\epsilon}})^{c}}\bigg]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\delta+\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}^{2}\bigg]\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\big(\bar{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{\delta}^{\epsilon})\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta.

The arbitrariness of
\delta
gives (
5.41
), which is first part of (4).

Choosing
m=\frac{\beta}{\beta+1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2m}\mathrm{d}t\bigg]

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}|\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t),\mathbf{Y}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\rangle|^{m}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}^{m}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{m}\mathrm{d}t\bigg]

(5.43)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}\mathrm{d}t\bigg]\bigg\}^{\frac{\beta-m}{\beta}}\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{\beta}\mathrm{d}t\bigg]\bigg\}^{\frac{m}{\beta}}.

Combining (
5.38
), (
5.40
) and (
5.41
), one can derive (
5.36
).
∎

Let us first prove the convergence of the control terms.

Lemma 5.8
.

For any
\boldsymbol{h}\in\mathbb{H}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\bigg\{\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)\big(\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s,\boldsymbol{h}\bigg)

(5.44)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\bigg\{\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\psi(s)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)\big(\varphi(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s,\boldsymbol{h}\bigg).

Proof.

Since
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}
converges to
\mathbf{Y}
in
\mathrm{D}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
strongly
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s., we obtain
\sup\limits_{s\in[0,T]}\|\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}<\infty,\ \bar{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s. Also since
L_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{H}_{2}
and
L_{\mathrm{B}}\in\mathrm{L}^{1}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{+})
, it follows from (
3.1
) and Lemma
5.4
that

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\bigg\{\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)\big(\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s,\boldsymbol{h}\bigg)

(5.45)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\bigg\{\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\psi(s)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)\big(\varphi(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s,\boldsymbol{h}\bigg).

For any
\delta>0
. Define

A_{\epsilon,\delta}:=\big\{s\in[0,T]:\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}>\delta\}
. From (
5.2
), we infer

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\lambda_{T}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A_{\epsilon,\delta}}}\right)}}\right]\leq\frac{1}{\delta^{2m}}\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2m}\mathrm{d}t\bigg]=0.

Therefore there exists a subsequence
\{\epsilon_{j}\}
(still denoted by the same index for simplicity) such that

(5.46)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\lambda_{T}(A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta})=0,\ \;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.s.}

Using Hypothesis
3.4
(H.7), (H.9) and Lemma
5.4
, we find

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\bigg\{\|\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t))-\mathrm{B}(t,\mathbf{Y}(t))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t),z)-\gamma(t,\mathbf{Y}(t),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\bigg\{\sqrt{L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)}\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\delta\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}^{c}}\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\big(L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)+\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}}\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\big(L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)+\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\delta\sup_{\boldsymbol{q}\in\mathcal{U}^{\Upsilon}}\bigg(\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}^{c}}\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\big(L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)+\|\psi(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|\varphi(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}t\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{q}\in\mathcal{U}^{\Upsilon}}\bigg(\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}}\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\big(L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)+\|\psi(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|\varphi(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}t\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\delta C_{L_{\mathrm{B}},L_{\gamma},\Upsilon}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{q}\in\mathcal{U}^{\Upsilon}}\bigg(\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}}\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\big(L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)+\|\psi(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)

(5.47)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|\varphi(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}t\bigg).

One should note that

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left.\times\sup_{\boldsymbol{q}\in\mathcal{U}^{\Upsilon}}\bigg(\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}}\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\big(L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)+\|\psi(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|\varphi(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}t\bigg)}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]}}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

(5.48)

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\boldsymbol{q}\in\mathcal{U}^{\Upsilon}}\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}}\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\big(L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)+\|\psi(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|\varphi(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{2}}}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Using the Dominated Convergence Theorem, (
5.46
), Lemmas
3.3
(3) and
5.4
, we obtain

(5.49)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\boldsymbol{q}\in\mathcal{U}^{\Upsilon}}\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}}\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\big(L_{\mathrm{B}}(t)+\|\psi(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(t,z)\big|\varphi(t,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{2}=0.

By (
5.38
), (
5.2
) and (
5.49
), we find

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\bigg\{\|\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t))-\mathrm{B}(t,\mathbf{Y}(t))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}

(5.50)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t),z)-\gamma(t,\mathbf{Y}(t),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}t\bigg]=0.

Hence, there exists a subsequence
\{\epsilon_{j}\}
(still denoted by the same index) such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\bigg\{\|\mathrm{B}(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t))-\mathrm{B}(t,\mathbf{Y}(t))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}

(5.51)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\gamma(t,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t),z)-\gamma(t,\mathbf{Y}(t),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}t=0,\;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.s.}

Combining (
5.2
) with (
5.2
), we obtain the required result (
5.8
).
∎

Let us fix

(5.52)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t):=\boldsymbol{x}+\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{\mathrm{A}}(s)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\psi(s)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)\big(\varphi(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s,

where
\widehat{\mathrm{A}}(\cdot)
is the same limit appearing in Lemma
5.7
. By the weak limit of (
5.1
), one can show that (cf.
[
41
]
)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t,\omega)=\bar{\mathbf{Y}}(t,\omega)=\mathbf{Y}(t,\omega),\text{ for }\mathrm{d}t\otimes\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.e. }(t,\omega).

For any
\Psi\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{+})
, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\mathbf{Y},\boldsymbol{q},\mathbf{X}):=

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\Psi(t)\int_{0}^{t}\bigg(\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\psi(s),\mathbf{X}(s)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)(\varphi(s,z)-1)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\mathbf{X}(s)\big)\bigg)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}t\bigg].

Lemma 5.9
.

The following limit holds true:

(5.53)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\mathcal{G}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}},\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon_{j}},\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}})=\mathcal{G}(\mathbf{Y},\boldsymbol{q},\mathbf{Y}).

Proof.

Using Lemma
3.1
and the fact that
\sup\limits_{s\in[0,T]}\|\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}<\infty,\;\bar{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s., we have for all
(t,\omega)\in[0,T]\times\Omega
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\Psi(t)\int_{0}^{t}\bigg(\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s),\mathbf{Y}(s)\big)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)\big(\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\mathbf{Y}(s)\bigg)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\Psi(t)\int_{0}^{t}\bigg(\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\psi(s),\mathbf{Y}(s)\big)\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)\big(\varphi(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\mathbf{Y}(s)\bigg)\mathrm{d}s.

Using Lemma
5.4
, we find

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{q}\in\mathcal{U}^{\Upsilon}}\bigg|\Psi(t)\int_{0}^{t}\bigg\{\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\psi(s),\mathbf{Y}(s)\big)+\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)\big(\varphi(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s,\mathbf{Y}(s)\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Psi}\sup_{\boldsymbol{q}\in\mathcal{U}^{\Upsilon}}\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\|\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\|\psi(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|\varphi(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Psi}\bigg(1+2\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg)\sup_{\boldsymbol{q}\in\mathcal{U}^{\Upsilon}}\bigg\{\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{T}\|\psi(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}(s,z)\big|\varphi(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Psi,L_{\gamma},\Upsilon,\mathfrak{b}}\bigg(1+2\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg),

where we have used a calculation similar to (
5.1
) for the first term and Hypothesis
3.4
(H.8) for the second term.
Applying the Dominated Convergence Theorem, we get

(5.54)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\mathcal{G}(\mathbf{Y},\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon_{j}},\mathbf{Y})=\mathcal{G}(\mathbf{Y},\boldsymbol{q},\mathbf{Y}).

For any
\delta>0
, recall

A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}:=\big\{s\in[0,T]:\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}>\delta\}
, and by (
5.46
) there exists a subsequence
\{\epsilon_{j}\}
(still denoted by the same index) such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\lambda_{T}(A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta})=0,\;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.s.}

Then, we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{G}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}},\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon_{j}},\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}})-\mathcal{G}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}},\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon_{j}},\mathbf{Y})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Psi}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}

(5.55)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big|\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg)\mathrm{d}s\bigg].

We consider the first term in the right hand side of the inequality (
5.2
) and estimate it using Hypothesis
1.1
(H.5), Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities as

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\sqrt{\mathfrak{b}(s)}\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\delta\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}^{c}}\sqrt{\mathfrak{b}(s)}\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}}\sqrt{\mathfrak{b}(s)}\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\delta\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\sup_{\psi\in\mathcal{U}^{\Upsilon}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\big\{\mathfrak{b}(s)+\|\psi(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big\}\mathrm{d}s\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}}}\right\}\sup_{\psi\in\widetilde{S}^{\Upsilon}}\bigg(\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}}\big\{\mathfrak{b}(s)+\|\psi(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big\}\mathrm{d}s\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\delta C_{\mathfrak{b},\Upsilon}+C\bigg\{1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{4}\bigg]\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{4}}\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{4}\bigg]\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{4}}

(5.56)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\psi\in\widetilde{S}^{\Upsilon}}\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}}\big\{\mathfrak{b}(s)+\|\psi(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big\}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{2}\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We estimate the final term in the right hand side of (
5.2
), using Hypothesis
3.4
(H.8), and Hölder’s inequality as

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big|\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(s,z)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\big|\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big|\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\delta\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}^{c}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(s,z)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\big|\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(s,z)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\big|\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big|\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\delta\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg(\sup_{\varphi\in S^{\Upsilon}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L_{\gamma}(s,z)\big|\varphi(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\big(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\bigg(\sup_{\varphi\in S^{\Upsilon}}\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(s,z)\big|\varphi(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\delta C_{L_{\gamma},\Upsilon}+C\bigg\{1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{4}\bigg]\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{4}}\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{4}\bigg]\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{4}}

(5.57)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\varphi\in S^{\Upsilon}}\int_{A_{\epsilon_{j},\delta}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}(s,z)\big|\varphi(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{2}\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Substituting (
5.2
) and (
5.2
) in (
5.2
), we obtain

(5.58)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\big|\mathcal{G}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}},\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon_{j}},\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}})-\mathcal{G}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}},\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon_{j}},\mathbf{Y})\big|=0.

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{G}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}},\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon_{j}},\mathbf{Y})-\mathcal{G}(\mathbf{Y},\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon_{j}},\mathbf{Y})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\bigg\{\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))-\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)-\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big|\|\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\bigg\{L_{\mathrm{B}}(s)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}

(5.59)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}R_{\gamma}(s,z)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big|\|\mathbf{Y}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s\bigg].

A similar calculation to (
5.58
) implies

(5.60)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\big|\mathcal{G}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}},\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon_{j}},\mathbf{Y})-\mathcal{G}(\mathbf{Y},\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon_{j}},\mathbf{Y})\big|=0.

Combining (
5.54
), (
5.58
) and (
5.60
), we obtain (
5.53
), which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.10
.

Since
\Psi\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{+})
is arbitrary, from Lemma
5.9
, along a further subsequence (still denoted by the same index for simplicity) one can obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\bigg\{\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\bigg(\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)\big(\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\bigg)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

(5.61)

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\bigg\{\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\psi(s),\mathbf{Y}(s)\big)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\bigg(\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)\big(\varphi(s,z)-1\big),\mathbf{Y}(s)\bigg)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s\bigg].

Our next aim is to identify the limit function
\widehat{\mathrm{A}}(\cdot)
. In view of Lemma
2.4
, it is enough to show the condition (
2.4
). Now, our goal is to verify the condition (
2.4
).

Lemma 5.11
.

We have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{A}}(\cdot)=\mathrm{A}(\cdot,\mathbf{Y}(\cdot)),\;\mathrm{d}t\otimes\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.e. }

Proof.

Applying infinite dimensional Itô’s formula to the process
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(\cdot)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}
(see Theorem 1.2,
[
32
]
), we get

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}-\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathrm{A}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\rangle+\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\bigg(\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)\big(\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\bigg)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon_{j}\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{\epsilon_{j}}\int_{0}^{t}\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\mathrm{d}\mathrm{W}(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon_{j}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s-),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}N^{\epsilon_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)

(5.62)

\displaystyle\quad+2\epsilon_{j}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\big(\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s-),z),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s-)\big)\widetilde{N}^{\epsilon_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z),\ \bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.s.},

for all
t\in[0,T]
. Notice that

\displaystyle M_{1}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\epsilon_{j}}\int_{0}^{t}\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\mathrm{d}\mathrm{W}(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\big),\ \text{ and }

\displaystyle M_{2}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t)

\displaystyle:=2\epsilon_{j}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\big(\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s-),z),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s-)\big)\widetilde{N}^{\epsilon_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z),

are square integrable martingales. Taking expectations on both sides of the expression (
5.2
), we find

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(T)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big]-\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\bigg\{\langle\mathrm{A}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\rangle\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+2\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\bigg\{\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\bigg(\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)\big(\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\bigg)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon_{j}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]+\epsilon_{j}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

(5.63)

\displaystyle=:\sum_{i=1}^{4}J_{i}(t).

Using Hypothesis
1.1
(H.5), Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
5.4
, we estimate the term
J_{3}(\cdot)
as

\displaystyle J_{3}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{j}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{j}\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)

(5.64)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{j}C.

Now, we consider
J_{4}(\cdot)
, and estimate it using Hypothesis
3.4
(H.8), Hölder’s inequality, Lemmas
3.1
and
3.3
as

\displaystyle J_{4}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{j}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L^{2}_{\gamma}(s,z)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)^{2}\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{j}\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg(\sup_{\varphi\in S^{\Upsilon}}\int_{0}^{T}L^{2}_{\gamma}(s,z)\varphi(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)

(5.65)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{j}C_{L_{\gamma},2,2,\Upsilon}.

Substituting (
5.2
) and (
5.2
) in (
5.2
), and using Fubini’s theorem and (
5.10
), we obtain

\displaystyle\liminf_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(T)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}-\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=2\liminf_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mathrm{A}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\rangle\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+2\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\bigg\{\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\psi(s),\mathbf{Y}(s)\big)

(5.66)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\bigg(\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)\big(\varphi(s,z)-1\big),\mathbf{Y}(s)\bigg)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s\bigg].

Taking the inner product with
\mathbf{Y}(\cdot)
in (
5.52
), we have

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|\mathbf{Y}(T)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+2\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\bigg\{\langle\widehat{\mathrm{A}}(t),\mathbf{Y}(s)\rangle+\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\psi(s),\mathbf{Y}(s)\big)

(5.67)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(t,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)\big(\varphi(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\mathbf{Y}(s)\bigg)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}s\bigg].

Using the fact that
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{H}}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{V}}
are lower semicontinuous in
\mathbb{V}^{*}
, the convergence
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(\omega)\to\mathbf{Y}({\omega})
in
\mathrm{D}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*})
and Fatou’s lemma, yield

(5.68)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|\mathbf{Y}(T)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big]-\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(T)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big]-\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}.

Combining (
5.2
)-(
5.68
), we arrive at

(5.69)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\langle\widehat{\mathrm{A}}(s),\mathbf{Y}(s)\rangle\mathrm{d}s\bigg]\leq\liminf_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mathrm{A}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)),\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\rangle\mathrm{d}s\bigg],

which verify the required condition (
2.4
) of Lemma
2.4
.
Therefore
\widehat{\mathrm{A}}(\cdot)=\mathrm{A}(\cdot,\mathbf{Y}(\cdot)),\;\mathrm{d}t\otimes\bar{\mathbb{P}}
-a.e., as required.
∎

Proposition 5.12
.

The process
\mathbf{Y}(\cdot,\omega)
solves the following equation:

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}(t,\omega)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{x}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{A}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s,\omega))\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s,\omega))\psi(s)\mathrm{d}s

(5.70)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s,\omega),z)\big(\varphi(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s,

which has a unique solution in
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{H})\cap\mathrm{L}^{\beta}(0,T;\mathbb{V})
, for
\beta\in(1,\infty)
.

Proof.

The proof follows from Lemmas
5.7
-
5.11
(see the proof of Theorem
4.1
also).
∎

For simplicity of notations, in this step, we suppress the dependency of the second variable in each of the processes.

Lemma 5.13
.

There exists a subsequence indexed by
\varpi_{j}
, such that the following holds:

(5.71)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varpi_{j}\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\varpi_{j}}(t)-\mathbf{Y}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}=0,\;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.s.}

Proof.

Setting
\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(\cdot)=\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(\cdot)-\mathbf{Y}(\cdot)
. Applying Itô’s formula to the process
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(\cdot)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathrm{A}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))-\mathrm{A}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s)),\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\rangle\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s)-\mathrm{B}(s,\mathbf{Y}(s))\psi(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\big)+\epsilon_{j}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\sqrt{\epsilon_{j}}\int_{0}^{t}\big(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\mathrm{d}\mathrm{W}(s),\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\big)

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\bigg(\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)\big(\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)-1\big)-\gamma(s,\mathbf{Y}(s),z)\big(\varphi(s,z)-1\big),\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\bigg)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\epsilon_{j}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\big(\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z),\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}\big)\widetilde{N}^{\epsilon_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon^{2}_{j}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}N^{\epsilon_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)

(5.72)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{7}J_{i}(t).

Since we know that
\lim\limits_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\mathrm{A}(\cdot,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(\cdot))=\widehat{\mathrm{A}}(\cdot)=\mathrm{A}(\cdot,\mathbf{Y}(\cdot)),\ \mathrm{d}t\otimes\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.e.}
, then

(5.73)

\displaystyle J_{1}(t)\to 0,\text{ as }\epsilon_{j}\to 0.

Using (
5.2
)-(
5.58
), we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\bigg(\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s)+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)\big(\varphi(s,z)-1\big)\nu(\mathrm{d}z),\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\bigg)\mathrm{d}s\bigg|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big|\varphi(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\sqrt{\mathfrak{b}(s)}\|\psi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}+\int_{\mathrm{Z}}L_{\gamma}\big|\varphi(s,z)-1\big|\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\big)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

(5.74)

\displaystyle\to 0\ \text{ as }\ \epsilon_{j}\to 0.

Thus, we obtain

(5.75)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{2}(t)|\bigg]=0\ \text{ and }\ \lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{5}(t)|\bigg]=0.

We can pass
\epsilon_{j}\to 0
in
J_{3}(\cdot)
and
J_{7}(\cdot)
as follows:

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{3}(t)|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{j}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{j}\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|^{2}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)

(5.76)

\displaystyle\to 0\ \text{ as }\ \epsilon_{j}\to 0,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{7}(t)|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{j}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{j}\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|^{2}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg(\sup_{\varphi\in S^{\Upsilon}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L^{2}_{\gamma}(s,z)\varphi(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)

(5.77)

\displaystyle\to 0\ \text{ as }\ \epsilon_{j}\to 0.

For
J_{4}(\cdot)
and
J_{6}(\cdot)
, we use the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy and Hölder’s inequalities as follows:

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{4}(t)|\bigg]

(5.78)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\epsilon_{j}}C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{B}(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}_{2}}^{2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\epsilon_{j}}C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\big(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\epsilon_{j}}C\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{b}(s)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg\{1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

(5.79)

\displaystyle\to 0\ \text{ as }\ \epsilon_{j}\to 0,

and

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|J_{6}(t)|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{j}C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}\|\gamma(s,\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s),z)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}N^{\epsilon_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon_{j}}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{j}C\bigg\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg\{\bigg(1+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(s)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\bigg(\sup_{\varphi\in S^{\Upsilon}}\int_{\mathrm{Z}_{T}}L^{2}_{\gamma}(s,z)\varphi(s,z)\nu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\bigg)\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

(5.80)

\displaystyle\to 0\ \text{ as }\ \epsilon_{j}\to 0.

Substituting (
5.73
)-(
5.2
) in (
5.2
), we obtain

(5.81)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon_{j}\to 0}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\epsilon_{j}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\bigg]=0.

Therefore, there exists a subsequence indexed by
\varpi_{j}
such that
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\varpi_{j}}
converges to
\mathbf{Y},\;\bar{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s., for all
t\in[0,T]
.
∎

5.3.
Verification of Condition
2.1
(2)

Let us now verify the condition
2.1
(2). First we recall (
5.3
), the solution representation.

Theorem 5.14
.

Fixed
\Upsilon\in\mathbb{N}
, and let
\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}=(\psi_{\epsilon},\varphi_{\epsilon})
and
\boldsymbol{q}=(\psi,\varphi)\in\mathcal{U}^{\Upsilon}
be such that
\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}
converges in distribution to
\boldsymbol{q}
as
\epsilon\to 0
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}^{\epsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\epsilon}\mathrm{W}(\cdot)+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\psi_{\epsilon}\mathrm{d}s,\epsilon N^{\epsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\epsilon}}}}\right)\Rightarrow\mathscr{G}^{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}\psi(s)\mathrm{d}s,\nu_{T}^{\varphi}}}\right).

Proof.

Recall
\bar{\mathbb{M}}
from Section
2
, and the notations from Proposition
5.6
. Denote

\displaystyle\Pi=\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\Upsilon},\mathrm{D}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*}),{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*}),{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*}),{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{V}^{*}),{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{H}_{1}),\bar{\mathbb{M}}\big),

where
\mathbb{H}_{1}
is a Hilbert space such that the embedding
\mathbb{H}\subset\mathbb{H}_{1}
is Hilbert-Schmidt.
From Proposition
5.6
, we know that the laws of
\big\{\big(\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon},M_{1}^{\epsilon},M_{2}^{\epsilon},X_{1}^{\epsilon},X_{2}^{\epsilon},X_{3}^{\epsilon},\mathrm{W},\bar{N}\big)\}
is tight in
\Pi
. Let
\big(\boldsymbol{q},0,0,X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},\mathrm{W},\bar{N}\big)
be any limit point of the family defined above. Using Skorokhod’s representation theorem (see Theorem A.1,
[
62
]
, or Theorem C.1,
[
15
]
), the construction of a new probability space is possible and we have the following convergences:

(5.82)

\displaystyle\big(\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon},M_{1}^{\epsilon},M_{2}^{\epsilon},X_{1}^{\epsilon},X_{2}^{\epsilon},X_{3}^{\epsilon},\mathrm{W},\bar{N}\big)\to\big(\boldsymbol{q},0,0,X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},\mathrm{W},\bar{N}\big),\text{ in }\Pi,\;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.s.}

Set
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)=\boldsymbol{x}+X_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)+X_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)+X_{3}^{\epsilon}(t)+M_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)+M_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)=\boldsymbol{x}+X_{1}(t)+X_{2}(t)+X_{3}(t)
. From the equation satisfied by the original processes, we may still assume that
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}
also satisfies (
5.1
).

Using that fact that if
f_{n}\in\mathrm{D}([0,T];\mathbb{R})
and
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}f_{n}=0
with the Skorokhod topology of
\mathrm{D}([0,T];\mathbb{R})
, then
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}|f_{n}(t)|=0
. From this fact and (
5.82
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|M_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}=0,\ \;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.s.},

and

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|M_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}=0,\ \;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.s.}

Moreover, we deduce

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X_{1}^{\epsilon}(t)-X_{1}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}=0,\ \;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.s.},

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X_{2}^{\epsilon}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}=0,\ \;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.s.},

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X_{3}^{\epsilon}(t)-X_{3}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}=0,\ \;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.s.},

and

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\epsilon}(t)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{V}^{*}}=0,\ \;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.s.}

Finally, using Proposition
5.12
and Lemma
5.13
, we can find the unique solution
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(\cdot)
of (
5.12
), and therefore there exists a subsequence indexed by
\varpi
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varpi\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\varpi}(t)-\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}(t)\|_{\mathbb{H}}=0,\ \;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{-a.s.},

which completes the proof of this theorem.
∎

We established the proof of verification of Condition
2.1
(1) in Proposition
4.2
and (2) in Theorem
5.14
. Therefore, the proof of Theorem
3.6
is completed.

5.4.
Applications

The LDP results obtained in this work are applicable to the stochastic versions of hydrodynamic models like (cf.
[
10
,
26
,
45
,
46
,
47
,
61
,
64
,
65
]
, etc.) Burgers equations, 2D Navier-Stokes equations, 2D magneto-hydrodynamic equations, 2D Boussinesq model for the Bénard convection, 2D Boussinesq system, 2D magnetic Bénard equations, 3D Leray-
\alpha
-model, the Ladyzhenskaya model, some shell models of turbulence like GOY, Sabra, dyadic, etc., porous media equations,
p
-Laplacian equations, fast-diffusion equations, power law fluids, Allen-Cahn equations, Kuramoto-Sivashinsky equations and 3D tamed Navier-Stokes equations. The paper
[

]
provided a detailed framework of the models, which comes under the mathematical setting of this work, like quasilinear SPDEs, convection-diffusion equations, Cahn-Hilliard equations, 2D Liquid crystal model, 2D Allen-Cahn-Navier-Stokes model, etc., and references therein.
